Most weeks, I’d say at least once. Like there’s usually one night where I eat like that, and then afterward I’m kind of panicking and trying to undo it somehow.

Sometimes that means I throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, sometimes I just don’t eat much the next day or I’ll make myself work out extra. It depends. Like if I have an early shift or I’m already wiped out from being under a truck all day, it might be more the skipping meals thing, but… yeah. It’s been happening for several months now, not just once or twice.

So… yeah, it feels pretty hard to stop once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll start thinking I’m just having something quick, and then once I’m going it’s like… my hands just keep reaching for stuff. I mean, stress is kind of always there, you know? Work gets backed up, someone needs a truck done yesterday, and then you get home and there’s still laundry sitting there from like three days ago.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’ve really connected it like, “Oh, I’m upset, so now I’m eating that way.” Sometimes I’m just tired, or bored, or I didn’t eat much earlier because the day got away from me. Or I’ll take laxatives. It’s embarrassing saying that out loud. I don’t know, it’s like this panic kicks in, and I’m thinking about all the food just sitting there. Then I do something stupid to try to make it feel less… out of control, I guess.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes. It’s not like every single time, but after I eat too much I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up because I get scared about what all that food is going to do to me.\n\nAnd then other times I’ll take laxatives instead… I know that’s not exactly what you asked, sorry. Like, once I start on those nights, it’s not like I’m calmly deciding, “Okay, one more thing.” It’s more like my hands just keep grabbing food, even while I’m telling myself, you know, stop, you’re done, you’re already feeling sick.\n\nAnd then I’ll notice stupid stuff, like the fridge door is still open, or I’m standing in the kitchen in my work pants with grease still on me from the shop, and I’m eating something I don’t even really want. So… yeah, it feels pretty hard to stop once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll start thinking I’m just having something quick, and then once I’m going it’s like… my hands just keep reaching for stuff. Even if part of me is saying, “Stop, you’re full, you feel sick,” I’m still opening cabinets or grabbing whatever’s there.\n\nIt’s weird because at work, like with engines and parts, I’m usually pretty controlled, you know? You mess up one thing and the whole job takes twice as long. But with food on those nights, it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I’m watching myself do it more than deciding how much I’m eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, stress is kind of always there, you know? Work gets backed up, someone needs a truck done yesterday, and then you get home and there’s still laundry sitting there from like three days ago.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’ve really connected it like, “Oh, I’m upset, so now I’m eating that way.” Sometimes I’m just tired, or bored, or I didn’t eat much earlier because the day got away from me. I guess I’d have to pay more attention to it. Right now it all kind of blends together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know, honestly. I don’t weigh myself much, and when I do, it’s kind of random—like at my mom’s place or if there’s a scale in a bathroom somewhere.\n\nMy work pants feel different sometimes, but they’re also always getting stretched out or dirty, and I’m crawling around under trucks all day, so it’s hard to tell. I’ve had times where I think I look heavier, and then other times I’m like, maybe I’m just tired or bloated or whatever.\n\nSo… I can’t say I’ve noticed some big clear change over time. I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating more than you planned happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it is pretty regular. Most weeks there’s at least one night where it happens, where I eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd then I’ll do something after to try to fix it, I guess—like throw up, or take laxatives, or skip meals the next day, or make myself work out more. It’s not every single night or anything, but it’s been going on for months now. I kind of kept telling myself it was just a bad week, but then it kept being another week.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up, take laxatives, or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Most weeks, I’d say at least once. Like there’s usually one night where I eat like that, and then afterward I’m kind of panicking and trying to undo it somehow.\n\nSometimes that means I throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, sometimes I just don’t eat much the next day or I’ll make myself work out extra. It depends. Like if I have an early shift or I’m already wiped out from being under a truck all day, it might be more the skipping meals thing, but… yeah. It’s been happening for several months now, not just once or twice.\n\nDoctor: Do you always try to undo the effects of these eating episodes by throwing up, taking laxatives, or skipping meals?\n\nPatient: Not always, no… I mean, sometimes I just sit there feeling gross and try to ignore it, but a lot of the time I get scared about what it’s going to do to me.\n\nLike, if I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or I’ll take laxatives. It’s embarrassing saying that out loud. I don’t know, it’s like this panic kicks in, and I’m thinking about all the food just sitting there. Then I do something stupid to try to make it feel less… out of control, I guess.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes. It’s not like every single time, but after I eat too much I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up because I get scared about what all that food is going to do to me.\n\nAnd then other times I’ll take laxatives instead… I know that’s not exactly what you asked, sorry. It’s just kind of all tied together in my head. But throwing up happens sometimes after those episodes.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically throw up or take laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Usually… I guess like once, maybe twice in a week? It’s not super scheduled or anything.\n\nSometimes after I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives because I’m scared of what the food’s going to do to me. It’s weird because I’ll be thinking about it while I’m trying to do normal stuff too, like at work, and then I’m not even paying attention to what I’m supposed to be fixing. But yeah… probably one or two times a week most weeks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Even if part of me is saying, “Stop, you’re full, you feel sick,” I’m still opening cabinets or grabbing whatever’s there.\n\nIt’s weird because at work, like with engines and parts, I’m usually pretty controlled, you know? You mess up one thing and the whole job takes twice as long. But with food on those nights, it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I’m watching myself do it more than deciding how much I’m eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, stress is kind of always there, you know? Work gets backed up, someone needs a truck done yesterday, and then you get home and there’s still laundry sitting there from like three days ago.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’ve really connected it like, “Oh, I’m upset, so now I’m eating that way.” Sometimes I’m just tired, or bored, or I didn’t eat much earlier because the day got away from me. I guess I’d have to pay more attention to it. Right now it all kind of blends together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know, honestly. I don’t weigh myself much, and when I do, it’s kind of random—like at my mom’s place or if there’s a scale in a bathroom somewhere.\n\nMy work pants feel different sometimes, but they’re also always getting stretched out or dirty, and I’m crawling around under trucks all day, so it’s hard to tell. I’ve had times where I think I look heavier, and then other times I’m like, maybe I’m just tired or bloated or whatever.\n\nSo… I can’t say I’ve noticed some big clear change over time. I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating more than you planned happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it is pretty regular. Most weeks there’s at least one night where it happens, where I eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd then I’ll do something after to try to fix it, I guess—like throw up, or take laxatives, or skip meals the next day, or make myself work out more. It’s not every single night or anything, but it’s been going on for months now. I kind of kept telling myself it was just a bad week, but then it kept being another week.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up, take laxatives, or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Most weeks, I’d say at least once. Like there’s usually one night where I eat like that, and then afterward I’m kind of panicking and trying to undo it somehow.\n\nSometimes that means I throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, sometimes I just don’t eat much the next day or I’ll make myself work out extra. It depends. Like if I have an early shift or I’m already wiped out from being under a truck all day, it might be more the skipping meals thing, but… yeah. It’s been happening for several months now, not just once or twice.\n\nDoctor: Do you always try to undo the effects of these eating episodes by throwing up, taking laxatives, or skipping meals?\n\nPatient: Not always, no… I mean, sometimes I just sit there feeling gross and try to ignore it, but a lot of the time I get scared about what it’s going to do to me.\n\nLike, if I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or I’ll take laxatives. It’s embarrassing saying that out loud. I don’t know, it’s like this panic kicks in, and I’m thinking about all the food just sitting there. Then I do something stupid to try to make it feel less… out of control, I guess.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes. It’s not like every single time, but after I eat too much I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up because I get scared about what all that food is going to do to me.\n\nAnd then other times I’ll take laxatives instead… I know that’s not exactly what you asked, sorry. It’s just kind of all tied together in my head. But throwing up happens sometimes after those episodes.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically throw up or take laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Usually… I guess like once, maybe twice in a week? It’s not super scheduled or anything.\n\nSometimes after I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives because I’m scared of what the food’s going to do to me. It’s weird because I’ll be thinking about it while I’m trying to do normal stuff too, like at work, and then I’m not even paying attention to what I’m supposed to be fixing. But yeah… probably one or two times a week most weeks.\n\nDoctor: Do you throw up or use laxatives after almost every time you eat too much, or is it less often than that?\n\nPatient: Less often than that, I think. It’s not after every time I eat too much.\n\nSometimes I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives because I start panicking about what the food is going to do to me. But other times I just… don’t. I might think about it a lot, like way too much, but I don’t always do it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Even if part of me is saying, “Stop, you’re full, you feel sick,” I’m still opening cabinets or grabbing whatever’s there.\n\nIt’s weird because at work, like with engines and parts, I’m usually pretty controlled, you know? You mess up one thing and the whole job takes twice as long. But with food on those nights, it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I’m watching myself do it more than deciding how much I’m eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, stress is kind of always there, you know? Work gets backed up, someone needs a truck done yesterday, and then you get home and there’s still laundry sitting there from like three days ago.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’ve really connected it like, “Oh, I’m upset, so now I’m eating that way.” Sometimes I’m just tired, or bored, or I didn’t eat much earlier because the day got away from me. I guess I’d have to pay more attention to it. Right now it all kind of blends together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know, honestly. I don’t weigh myself much, and when I do, it’s kind of random—like at my mom’s place or if there’s a scale in a bathroom somewhere.\n\nMy work pants feel different sometimes, but they’re also always getting stretched out or dirty, and I’m crawling around under trucks all day, so it’s hard to tell. I’ve had times where I think I look heavier, and then other times I’m like, maybe I’m just tired or bloated or whatever.\n\nSo… I can’t say I’ve noticed some big clear change over time. I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating more than you planned happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it is pretty regular. Most weeks there’s at least one night where it happens, where I eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd then I’ll do something after to try to fix it, I guess—like throw up, or take laxatives, or skip meals the next day, or make myself work out more. It’s not every single night or anything, but it’s been going on for months now. I kind of kept telling myself it was just a bad week, but then it kept being another week.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up, take laxatives, or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Most weeks, I’d say at least once. Like there’s usually one night where I eat like that, and then afterward I’m kind of panicking and trying to undo it somehow.\n\nSometimes that means I throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, sometimes I just don’t eat much the next day or I’ll make myself work out extra. It depends. Like if I have an early shift or I’m already wiped out from being under a truck all day, it might be more the skipping meals thing, but… yeah. It’s been happening for several months now, not just once or twice.\n\nDoctor: Do you always try to undo the effects of these eating episodes by throwing up, taking laxatives, or skipping meals?\n\nPatient: Not always, no… I mean, sometimes I just sit there feeling gross and try to ignore it, but a lot of the time I get scared about what it’s going to do to me.\n\nLike, if I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or I’ll take laxatives. It’s embarrassing saying that out loud. I don’t know, it’s like this panic kicks in, and I’m thinking about all the food just sitting there. Then I do something stupid to try to make it feel less… out of control, I guess.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes. It’s not like every single time, but after I eat too much I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up because I get scared about what all that food is going to do to me.\n\nAnd then other times I’ll take laxatives instead… I know that’s not exactly what you asked, sorry. It’s just kind of all tied together in my head. But throwing up happens sometimes after those episodes.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically throw up or take laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Usually… I guess like once, maybe twice in a week? It’s weird because I’ll be thinking about it while I’m trying to do normal stuff too, like at work, and then I’m not even paying attention to what I’m supposed to be fixing. But yeah… probably one or two times a week most weeks.\n\nDoctor: Do you throw up or use laxatives after almost every time you eat too much, or is it less often than that?\n\nPatient: Less often than that, I think. But other times I just… don’t. I might think about it a lot, like way too much, but I don’t always do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you throw up or use laxatives after most of the times you eat too much?\n\nPatient: No, not most times. I don’t know, it sounds stupid saying it out loud, but it’s been happening enough that I figured I should probably talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these episodes of eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess sometimes I feel pretty bad about it, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. It’s more like I sit there thinking, “Why did I do that again?” and then I try not to think about it.\n\nLike, I’ll get distracted cleaning up wrappers or checking my phone, and then I remember I still have to get up early for work, and it kind of all runs together. So… yeah, maybe? I’m not totally sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s probably the part that freaks me out the most. Like, once I start on those nights, it’s not like I’m calmly deciding, “Okay, one more thing.” It’s more like my hands just keep grabbing food, even while I’m telling myself, you know, stop, you’re done, you’re already feeling sick.\n\nAnd then I’ll notice stupid stuff, like the fridge door is still open, or I’m standing in the kitchen in my work pants with grease still on me from the shop, and I’m eating something I don’t even really want. So… yeah, it feels pretty hard to stop once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll start thinking I’m just having something quick, and then once I’m going it’s like… my hands just keep reaching for stuff. Even if part of me is saying, “Stop, you’re full, you feel sick,” I’m still opening cabinets or grabbing whatever’s there.\n\nIt’s weird because at work, like with engines and parts, I’m usually pretty controlled, you know? You mess up one thing and the whole job takes twice as long. But with food on those nights, it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I’m watching myself do it more than deciding how much I’m eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, stress is kind of always there, you know? Work gets backed up, someone needs a truck done yesterday, and then you get home and there’s still laundry sitting there from like three days ago.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’ve really connected it like, “Oh, I’m upset, so now I’m eating that way.” Sometimes I’m just tired, or bored, or I didn’t eat much earlier because the day got away from me. I guess I’d have to pay more attention to it. Right now it all kind of blends together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know, honestly. I don’t weigh myself much, and when I do, it’s kind of random—like at my mom’s place or if there’s a scale in a bathroom somewhere.\n\nMy work pants feel different sometimes, but they’re also always getting stretched out or dirty, and I’m crawling around under trucks all day, so it’s hard to tell. I’ve had times where I think I look heavier, and then other times I’m like, maybe I’m just tired or bloated or whatever.\n\nSo… I can’t say I’ve noticed some big clear change over time. I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating more than you planned happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it is pretty regular. Most weeks there’s at least one night where it happens, where I eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd then I’ll do something after to try to fix it, I guess—like throw up, or take laxatives, or skip meals the next day, or make myself work out more. It’s not every single night or anything, but it’s been going on for months now. I kind of kept telling myself it was just a bad week, but then it kept being another week.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up, take laxatives, or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Most weeks, I’d say at least once. Like there’s usually one night where I eat like that, and then afterward I’m kind of panicking and trying to undo it somehow.\n\nSometimes that means I throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, sometimes I just don’t eat much the next day or I’ll make myself work out extra. It depends. Like if I have an early shift or I’m already wiped out from being under a truck all day, it might be more the skipping meals thing, but… yeah. It’s been happening for several months now, not just once or twice.\n\nDoctor: Do you always try to undo the effects of these eating episodes by throwing up, taking laxatives, or skipping meals?\n\nPatient: Not always, no… I mean, sometimes I just sit there feeling gross and try to ignore it, but a lot of the time I get scared about what it’s going to do to me.\n\nLike, if I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or I’ll take laxatives. It’s embarrassing saying that out loud. I don’t know, it’s like this panic kicks in, and I’m thinking about all the food just sitting there. Then I do something stupid to try to make it feel less… out of control, I guess.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes. It’s not like every single time, but after I eat too much I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up because I get scared about what all that food is going to do to me.\n\nAnd then other times I’ll take laxatives instead… I know that’s not exactly what you asked, sorry. It’s just kind of all tied together in my head. But throwing up happens sometimes after those episodes.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically throw up or take laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Usually… I guess like once, maybe twice in a week? It’s not super scheduled or anything.\n\nSometimes after I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives because I’m scared of what the food’s going to do to me. It’s weird because I’ll be thinking about it while I’m trying to do normal stuff too, like at work, and then I’m not even paying attention to what I’m supposed to be fixing. But yeah… probably one or two times a week most weeks.\n\nDoctor: Do you throw up or use laxatives after almost every time you eat too much, or is it less often than that?\n\nPatient: Less often than that, I think. It’s not after every time I eat too much.\n\nSometimes I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives because I start panicking about what the food is going to do to me. But other times I just… don’t. I might think about it a lot, like way too much, but I don’t always do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you throw up or use laxatives after most of the times you eat too much?\n\nPatient: No, not most times. I mean… sometimes after I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives because I get scared about what the food is going to do to me.\n\nBut it’s not like every single time or most of the time. It kind of depends, I guess. Like if I’m already stressed or if I’m thinking about how my clothes feel or whatever… sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, sometimes, not most times.\n\nDoctor: Do you use laxatives more often than you throw up after eating too much?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s always one more than the other… I mean, sometimes if I’ve eaten too much I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, and other times I’ll take laxatives because I’m scared about what the food’s going to do to me.\n\nIt kind of depends what I have around or what I’m thinking in the moment. Like if I’m at home it’s different than if I’m out, or if I’m already worked up about it. Sorry, I’m kind of circling it. I guess I do both sometimes, not really one clearly more than the other.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.8, F50.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Uh, yeah… I guess I’m here because my eating’s been kind of out of control lately. Like after a long shift at the shop, or if I’ve been studying, I’ll tell myself I’m just grabbing dinner, but then it turns into a full fast-food meal, snacks, and then I’m still digging through whatever’s in the fridge when I get home.\n\nIt’s like all in one sitting, way more than I planned. I don’t know, it sounds stupid saying it out loud, but it’s been happening enough that I figured I should probably talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these episodes of eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess sometimes I feel pretty bad about it, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. It’s more like I sit there thinking, “Why did I do that again?” and then I try not to think about it.\n\nLike, I’ll get distracted cleaning up wrappers or checking my phone, and then I remember I still have to get up early for work, and it kind of all runs together. So… yeah, maybe? I’m not totally sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s probably the part that freaks me out the most. Like, once I start on those nights, it’s not like I’m calmly deciding, “Okay, one more thing.” It’s more like my hands just keep grabbing food, even while I’m telling myself, you know, stop, you’re done, you’re already feeling sick.\n\nAnd then I’ll notice stupid stuff, like the fridge door is still open, or I’m standing in the kitchen in my work pants with grease still on me from the shop, and I’m eating something I don’t even really want. So… yeah, it feels pretty hard to stop once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll start thinking I’m just having something quick, and then once I’m going it’s like… my hands just keep reaching for stuff. Even if part of me is saying, “Stop, you’re full, you feel sick,” I’m still opening cabinets or grabbing whatever’s there.\n\nIt’s weird because at work, like with engines and parts, I’m usually pretty controlled, you know? You mess up one thing and the whole job takes twice as long. But with food on those nights, it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I’m watching myself do it more than deciding how much I’m eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, stress is kind of always there, you know? Work gets backed up, someone needs a truck done yesterday, and then you get home and there’s still laundry sitting there from like three days ago.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’ve really connected it like, “Oh, I’m upset, so now I’m eating that way.” Sometimes I’m just tired, or bored, or I didn’t eat much earlier because the day got away from me. I guess I’d have to pay more attention to it. Right now it all kind of blends together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know, honestly. I don’t weigh myself much, and when I do, it’s kind of random—like at my mom’s place or if there’s a scale in a bathroom somewhere.\n\nMy work pants feel different sometimes, but they’re also always getting stretched out or dirty, and I’m crawling around under trucks all day, so it’s hard to tell. I’ve had times where I think I look heavier, and then other times I’m like, maybe I’m just tired or bloated or whatever.\n\nSo… I can’t say I’ve noticed some big clear change over time. I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating more than you planned happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it is pretty regular. Most weeks there’s at least one night where it happens, where I eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd then I’ll do something after to try to fix it, I guess—like throw up, or take laxatives, or skip meals the next day, or make myself work out more. It’s not every single night or anything, but it’s been going on for months now. I kind of kept telling myself it was just a bad week, but then it kept being another week.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up, take laxatives, or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Most weeks, I’d say at least once. Like there’s usually one night where I eat like that, and then afterward I’m kind of panicking and trying to undo it somehow.\n\nSometimes that means I throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, sometimes I just don’t eat much the next day or I’ll make myself work out extra. It depends. Like if I have an early shift or I’m already wiped out from being under a truck all day, it might be more the skipping meals thing, but… yeah. It’s been happening for several months now, not just once or twice.\n\nDoctor: Do you always try to undo the effects of these eating episodes by throwing up, taking laxatives, or skipping meals?\n\nPatient: Not always, no… I mean, sometimes I just sit there feeling gross and try to ignore it, but a lot of the time I get scared about what it’s going to do to me.\n\nLike, if I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or I’ll take laxatives. It’s embarrassing saying that out loud. I don’t know, it’s like this panic kicks in, and I’m thinking about all the food just sitting there. Then I do something stupid to try to make it feel less… out of control, I guess.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes. It’s not like every single time, but after I eat too much I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up because I get scared about what all that food is going to do to me.\n\nAnd then other times I’ll take laxatives instead… I know that’s not exactly what you asked, sorry. It’s just kind of all tied together in my head. But throwing up happens sometimes after those episodes.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically throw up or take laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Usually… I guess like once, maybe twice in a week? It’s not super scheduled or anything.\n\nSometimes after I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives because I’m scared of what the food’s going to do to me. It’s weird because I’ll be thinking about it while I’m trying to do normal stuff too, like at work, and then I’m not even paying attention to what I’m supposed to be fixing. But yeah… probably one or two times a week most weeks.\n\nDoctor: Do you throw up or use laxatives after almost every time you eat too much, or is it less often than that?\n\nPatient: Less often than that, I think. But other times I just… don’t. I might think about it a lot, like way too much, but I don’t always do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you throw up or use laxatives after most of the times you eat too much?\n\nPatient: No, not most times. Yes, sometimes it’s more than twice a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you throw up or use laxatives after eating too much every day?\n\nPatient: Not every day, no. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Almost every day… no, I don’t think it’s that often. I mean, sometimes after I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives because I get scared about what the food is going to do to me.

But almost every day sounds… more than it is, I think. Like, there are stretches where it happens a lot, especially if I’m already stressed, but not every day or almost every day.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Uh, yeah… I guess I’m here because my eating’s been kind of out of control lately. Like after a long shift at the shop, or if I’ve been studying, I’ll tell myself I’m just grabbing dinner, but then it turns into a full fast-food meal, snacks, and then I’m still digging through whatever’s in the fridge when I get home.\n\nIt’s like all in one sitting, way more than I planned. I don’t know, it sounds stupid saying it out loud, but it’s been happening enough that I figured I should probably talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these episodes of eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess sometimes I feel pretty bad about it, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. It’s more like I sit there thinking, “Why did I do that again?” and then I try not to think about it.\n\nLike, I’ll get distracted cleaning up wrappers or checking my phone, and then I remember I still have to get up early for work, and it kind of all runs together. So… yeah, maybe? I’m not totally sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s probably the part that freaks me out the most. Like, once I start on those nights, it’s not like I’m calmly deciding, “Okay, one more thing.” It’s more like my hands just keep grabbing food, even while I’m telling myself, you know, stop, you’re done, you’re already feeling sick.\n\nAnd then I’ll notice stupid stuff, like the fridge door is still open, or I’m standing in the kitchen in my work pants with grease still on me from the shop, and I’m eating something I don’t even really want. So… yeah, it feels pretty hard to stop once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll start thinking I’m just having something quick, and then once I’m going it’s like… my hands just keep reaching for stuff. You mess up one thing and the whole job takes twice as long. But with food on those nights, it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like I’m watching myself do it more than deciding how much I’m eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, stress is kind of always there, you know? Work gets backed up, someone needs a truck done yesterday, and then you get home and there’s still laundry sitting there from like three days ago.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’ve really connected it like, “Oh, I’m upset, so now I’m eating that way.” Sometimes I’m just tired, or bored, or I didn’t eat much earlier because the day got away from me. I guess I’d have to pay more attention to it. Right now it all kind of blends together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know, honestly. I don’t weigh myself much, and when I do, it’s kind of random—like at my mom’s place or if there’s a scale in a bathroom somewhere.\n\nMy work pants feel different sometimes, but they’re also always getting stretched out or dirty, and I’m crawling around under trucks all day, so it’s hard to tell. I’ve had times where I think I look heavier, and then other times I’m like, maybe I’m just tired or bloated or whatever.\n\nSo… I can’t say I’ve noticed some big clear change over time. I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating more than you planned happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it is pretty regular. Most weeks there’s at least one night where it happens, where I eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd then I’ll do something after to try to fix it, I guess—like throw up, or take laxatives, or skip meals the next day, or make myself work out more. It’s not every single night or anything, but it’s been going on for months now. I kind of kept telling myself it was just a bad week, but then it kept being another week.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up, take laxatives, or skip meals after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Most weeks, I’d say at least once. Like there’s usually one night where I eat like that, and then afterward I’m kind of panicking and trying to undo it somehow.\n\nSometimes that means I throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, sometimes I just don’t eat much the next day or I’ll make myself work out extra. It depends. Like if I have an early shift or I’m already wiped out from being under a truck all day, it might be more the skipping meals thing, but… yeah. It’s been happening for several months now, not just once or twice.\n\nDoctor: Do you always try to undo the effects of these eating episodes by throwing up, taking laxatives, or skipping meals?\n\nPatient: Not always, no… I mean, sometimes I just sit there feeling gross and try to ignore it, but a lot of the time I get scared about what it’s going to do to me.\n\nLike, if I eat too much, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or I’ll take laxatives. It’s embarrassing saying that out loud. I don’t know, it’s like this panic kicks in, and I’m thinking about all the food just sitting there. Then I do something stupid to try to make it feel less… out of control, I guess.\n\nDoctor: How often do you throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Sometimes. It’s not like every single time, but after I eat too much I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up because I get scared about what all that food is going to do to me.\n\nAnd then other times I’ll take laxatives instead… I know that’s not exactly what you asked, sorry. But other times I just… don’t.
